{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_7f4869726e4640c6b9d7570d","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Report usage across every tool round, not just the last\n2\t\n3\tIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#93\n4\tLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:S, area:api\n5\t\n6\t## Why\n7\t\n8\t`afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n9\t\n10\tFor a turn that answered directly, that is the whole turn. For a turn that called tools, it is the last leg of several, and the earlier rounds — including the one carrying the customer's question and the full tool results — are not counted anywhere. A deployer billing or budgeting from this hook under-counts exactly the expensive turns, and nothing in the signature hints at it. The current behaviour is recorded in `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` so it is at least discoverable, but documenting a surprising number is weaker than not producing one.\n11\t\n12\tRaised by the reviewer on HALEngine#83, which recorded the behaviour but could not change it: that PR's ticket scoped itself to specifying the seam, not altering the orchestrator.\n13\t\n14\t## What to do\n15\t\n16\tDecide, and make the signature say the answer. Two shapes are worth weighing, and the issue should record why the other was rejected:\n17\t\n18\t- Accumulate across rounds and pass the sum, keeping one `usage` argument. Simple for the common case, and it silently changes what an existing consumer records — the number gets larger for tool-heavy turns, which is the point, but it is a behaviour change worth a MINOR bump and a changelog line that says so.\n19\t- Pass both, for example the summed total plus the per-round breakdown, so a consumer that wants provider-call granularity keeps it. More faithful, wider surface.\n20\t\n21\tWhichever is chosen, a turn that ran no tools must report exactly what it reports today, so the common path does not move.\n22\t\n23\t## Acceptance criteria\n24\t\n25\t- [ ] A turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call in that turn, asserted against a scripted provider whose rounds report known, different token counts\n26\t- [ ] A turn with no tool calls reports exactly what it reports today, pinned by the existing test remaining unedited\n27\t- [ ] A provider that reports no usage on some rounds does not make the total `undefined` or `NaN`: the rounds that reported are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported still reports nothing\n28\t- [ ] The type of what the hook receives makes the answer unambiguous, so a reader does not have to open the orchestrator to learn whether it is a sum\n29\t- [ ] `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` replaces its recorded under-counting line with the new behaviour, and the CHANGELOG entry states the change in what an existing consumer will record\n30\t- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`) and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the public API\n31\t\n32\t## Tech notes\n33\t\n34\tOut of scope: the audit entry or billing record itself, which is a consumer's concern; token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes; and the `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape, which providers already populate and this issue only aggregates.\n35\t\n36\tKey files: `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` (the `lastUsage` local and the `afterModelResponse` call site), `src/types/ai.ts` (`UsageMetadata`), `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md`.\n37\t\n38\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"04691e8d-bf30-4680-addf-c60a90bc5e36","uuid":"326d8074-61e4-449d-9e4d-fd7fcfbc0d0e","timestamp":"2026-11-02T18:57:14.705Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/d7d43383fd97/workspace/ticket.md","content":"# Report usage across every tool round, not just the last\n\nIssue: re-cinq/HALEngine#93\nLabels: priority:medium, type:feature, size:S, area:api\n\n## Why\n\n`afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n\nFor a turn that answered directly, that is the whole turn. For a turn that called tools, it is the last leg of several, and the earlier rounds — including the one carrying the customer's question and the full tool results — are not counted anywhere. A deployer billing or budgeting from this hook under-counts exactly the expensive turns, and nothing in the signature hints at it. The current behaviour is recorded in `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` so it is at least discoverable, but documenting a surprising number is weaker than not producing one.\n\nRaised by the reviewer on HALEngine#83, which recorded the behaviour but could not change it: that PR's ticket scoped itself to specifying the seam, not altering the orchestrator.\n\n## What to do\n\nDecide, and make the signature say the answer. Two shapes are worth weighing, and the issue should record why the other was rejected:\n\n- Accumulate across rounds and pass the sum, keeping one `usage` argument. Simple for the common case, and it silently changes what an existing consumer records — the number gets larger for tool-heavy turns, which is the point, but it is a behaviour change worth a MINOR bump and a changelog line that says so.\n- Pass both, for example the summed total plus the per-round breakdown, so a consumer that wants provider-call granularity keeps it. More faithful, wider surface.\n\nWhichever is chosen, a turn that ran no tools must report exactly what it reports today, so the common path does not move.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A turn driven through two tool rounds reports usage covering every provider call in that turn, asserted against a scripted provider whose rounds report known, different token counts\n- [ ] A turn with no tool calls reports exactly what it reports today, pinned by the existing test remaining unedited\n- [ ] A provider that reports no usage on some rounds does not make the total `undefined` or `NaN`: the rounds that reported are still counted, and a turn where nothing reported still reports nothing\n- [ ] The type of what the hook receives makes the answer unambiguous, so a reader does not have to open the orchestrator to learn whether it is a sum\n- [ ] `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md` replaces its recorded under-counting line with the new behaviour, and the CHANGELOG entry states the change in what an existing consumer will record\n- [ ] `npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run eslint` (`--max-warnings 0`) and `npm run prettier:check` pass, with no `any` in the public API\n\n## Tech notes\n\nOut of scope: the audit entry or billing record itself, which is a consumer's concern; token accounting for anything other than the provider calls the orchestrator makes; and the `stop` chunk's own `usage` shape, which providers already populate and this issue only aggregates.\n\nKey files: `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` (the `lastUsage` local and the `afterModelResponse` call site), `src/types/ai.ts` (`UsageMetadata`), `specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md`.\n\n","numLines":38,"startLine":1,"totalLines":38}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_7f4869726e4640c6b9d7570d","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_a24251fffcf84916a22622a7","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Definition of Done\n2\t\n3\t> `afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n4\t\n5\t**Strategy: `direct`** — `createChatOrchestrator` already exposes the running `afterModelResponse` hook, so scripted provider rounds can verify its reported usage through the real tool loop. The existing single-round hook test remains the unchanged no-tool regression guard.\n6\t\n7\t## Done when these pass\n8\t\n9\t- [ ] **reports the summed usage from every provider call in a turn with two tool rounds** — `afterModelResponse` receives the total of all three provider calls made by a two-tool-round turn.\n10\t      `src/orchestration/chatOrchestratorUsage.acceptance.test.ts`\n11\t- [ ] **counts reported rounds while leaving a turn with no reported usage undefined** — missing round usage does not poison reported totals, while an entirely unreported turn remains undefined.\n12\t      `src/orchestration/chatOrchestratorUsage.acceptance.test.ts`\n13\t\n14\t## Facets\n15\t\n16\t- [ ] Replace final-round retention with a deliberately named total-usage contract whose public type makes aggregation explicit.\n17\t- [ ] Sum each token field only for provider rounds that report usage, preserving `undefined` when none do.\n18\t- [ ] Preserve the unchanged no-tool regression guard, `receives collected response text and usage`, in `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts`.\n19\t- [ ] Update the hooks spec and CHANGELOG with the aggregate-usage behavior and its impact on existing consumers.\n20\t\n21\t## Out of scope\n22\t\n23\t- Audit or billing records created by consumers, non-provider token accounting, and the provider `stop` chunk usage shape.\n24\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"04691e8d-bf30-4680-addf-c60a90bc5e36","uuid":"d3bc3196-ef75-435d-8fa6-e592361e1266","timestamp":"2026-11-02T18:57:20.502Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/d7d43383fd97/workspace/target/.lore/dod.md","content":"# Definition of Done\n\n> `afterModelResponse` is the hook a deployer reaches for when they need to record what a turn cost — an audit entry, a billing line, a usage budget. It is called once per user message, after the round loop exits, and is handed the `lastUsage` local: `chatOrchestrator.ts` assigns `lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage` on each round, so what the hook receives is the usage of the final provider call alone.\n\n**Strategy: `direct`** — `createChatOrchestrator` already exposes the running `afterModelResponse` hook, so scripted provider rounds can verify its reported usage through the real tool loop. The existing single-round hook test remains the unchanged no-tool regression guard.\n\n## Done when these pass\n\n- [ ] **reports the summed usage from every provider call in a turn with two tool rounds** — `afterModelResponse` receives the total of all three provider calls made by a two-tool-round turn.\n      `src/orchestration/chatOrchestratorUsage.acceptance.test.ts`\n- [ ] **counts reported rounds while leaving a turn with no reported usage undefined** — missing round usage does not poison reported totals, while an entirely unreported turn remains undefined.\n      `src/orchestration/chatOrchestratorUsage.acceptance.test.ts`\n\n## Facets\n\n- [ ] Replace final-round retention with a deliberately named total-usage contract whose public type makes aggregation explicit.\n- [ ] Sum each token field only for provider rounds that report usage, preserving `undefined` when none do.\n- [ ] Preserve the unchanged no-tool regression guard, `receives collected response text and usage`, in `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts`.\n- [ ] Update the hooks spec and CHANGELOG with the aggregate-usage behavior and its impact on existing consumers.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n- Audit or billing records created by consumers, non-provider token accounting, and the provider `stop` chunk usage shape.\n","numLines":24,"startLine":1,"totalLines":24}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_a24251fffcf84916a22622a7","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

from '../shared/logger.js';\n27\t\n28\tconst DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5;\n29\t\n30\texport interface OrchestratorHooks {\n31\t  beforeSession?: (session: ChatSession) => Promise<void>;\n32\t  afterSession?: (session: ChatSession) => Promise<void>;\n33\t  beforeUserInput?: (session: ChatSession, userMessage: string) => Promise<string>;\n34\t  afterUserInput?: (session: ChatSession, string, usage?: UsageMetadata) => Promise<void>;\n37\t  onError?: (session: ChatSession, error: Error) => Promise<void>;\n38\t  /** The supported seam for an EU AI Act Art. 14 human-oversight control: fires before each known tool's executor, and a returned ToolResponse replaces the call (see docs/adding-a-tool.md); it receives the model's raw tool input and, through session, the caller's authHeaders, so a policy that logs either logs personal data and credential material; the engine asserts nothing about any policy installed here. */\n39\t  beforeToolCall?: (session: ChatSession, call: ToolCall) => Promise<ToolResponse | undefined>;\n40\t  /** Fires when the model asks for a tool round the budget refuses, after the last provider call and before afterModelResponse; a returned = NonNullable<OrchestratorHooks['beforeToolCall']>;\n50\ttype OnToolBudgetExhausted = NonNullable<OrchestratorHooks['onToolBudgetExhausted']>;\n51\t\n52\texport const TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED = 'tool_budget_exhausted';\n53\t\n54\texport interface ChatOrchestrator {\n55\t  processMessage(session: ChatSession): Promise<string>;\n56\t  processMessageStream(session: ChatSession): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk>;\n57\t}\n58\t\n59\texport interface ChatOrchestratorOptions {\n60\t  maxToolRounds?: number;\n61\t  contextConfig?: Partial<ContextConfig>;\n62\t  promptBuilderOptions?: suppressOutput: boolean;\n70\t}\n71\texport function createChatOrchestrator(\n72\t  provider: AIProvider,\n73\t  promptBuilder: PromptBuilder,\n74\t  toolRegistry?: ToolRegistry,\n75\t  options?: promptBuilder.build({...options?.promptBuilderOptions, toolInstructions});\n81\t  const tools = toolRegistry?.getDefinitions();\n82\t  const hooks = options?.hooks;\n83\t  const sessionStore = const systemPrompt = hooks?.beforeModelResponse\n103\t          ? await hooks.beforeModelResponse(session, baseSystemPrompt)\n104\t          : baseSystemPrompt;\n105\t\n106\t        const messages: log.warn('orchestrator', 'tool budget exhausted', {\n177\t    maxToolRounds,\n178\t    requestedTools: pendingToolCalls.map(tc => tc.name),\n179\t  });\n180\t  return true;\n181\t}\n182\t\n183\t// The stop always follows the text: the round's own stop already committed its entry, and a bare text chunk would open one never committed.\n184\tasync function* closeExhaustedTurn(\n185\t  session: undefined) ?? '';\n190\t  if (sentence !== '') yield {type: 'text', text: sentence};\n191\t  yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED};\n192\t  return sentence;\n193\t}\n194\t\n195\tinterface RoundOutcome {\n196\t  stopReason: string;\n197\t  text: string;\n198\t  usage?: UsageMetadata;\n199\t}\n200\t\n201\t// `yield*` in the caller evaluates to this return, so the round's totals come back as data.\n202\tasync function* streamRound(\n203\t  provider: AIProvider,\n204\t  request: SendMessageParams,\n205\t  pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[]\n206\t): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk, RoundOutcome> {\n207\t  let stopReason = '';\n208\t  let text = '';\n209\t  let usage: UsageMetadata | undefined;\n210\t\n211\t  for await (const chunk of provider.sendMessage(request)) {\n212\t    yield chunk;\n213\t    collectToolCalls(chunk, pendingToolCalls);\n214\t    stopReason = extractStopReason(chunk) ?? await consultPolicy(beforeToolCall, session, tc) : undefined;\n265\t    return declined ?? toolRegistry.execute(tc.name, tc.input, context);\n266\t  };\n267\t}\n268\t\n269\t// A throwing policy declines its call rather than failing the turn: every tool_use must still be answered.\n270\tasync function consultPolicy(\n271\t  policy: BeforeToolCall,\n272\t  session: ChatSession,\n273\t  tc: ToolCall\n274\t): Promise<ToolResponse | undefined> {\n275\t  try {\n276\t    return await policy(session, tc);\n277\t  } catch (error) {\n278\t    log.warn('orchestrator', 'beforeToolCall threw, so the call is declined', {\n279\t      tool: tc.name,\n280\t      errorType: error instanceof Error ? type {ChatSession} from '../types/session.js';\nimport type {ToolRegistry, ToolContext, ToolResponse} from './tools/registry.js';\nimport type {\n  AIProvider,\n  Message,\n  MessageChunk,\n  SendMessageParams,\n  ToolCall,\n  ToolResultContent,\n  UsageMetadata,\n} from '../types/ai.js';\nimport type {OutgoingMessage} from '../types/messages.js';\nimport type {PromptBuilder, PromptBuilderOptions} from '../infrastructure/builders/promptBuilder.js';\nimport type {SessionStore} from '../types/sessionStore.js';\nimport {toMessages, createContextConfig} from './conversationContext.js';\nimport type {ContextConfig} from './conversationContext.js';\nimport {\n  shouldContinueToolLoop,\n  collectText,\n  buildToolCallMessages,\n  collectToolCalls,\n  extractStopReason,\n  extractUsage,\n} from './orchestratorHelpers.js';\nimport {appendEntry, commitStreamingEntries} from './entryMutations.js';\nimport {log} from '../shared/logger.js';\n\nconst DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 5;\n\nexport interface OrchestratorHooks {\n  beforeSession?: (session: ChatSession) => Promise<void>;\n  afterSession?: (session: ChatSession) => Promise<void>;\n  beforeUserInput?: (session: ChatSession, userMessage: string) => Promise<string>;\n  afterUserInput?: (session: ChatSession, userMessage: string) => Promise<void>;\n  beforeModelResponse?: (session: ChatSession, systemPrompt: string) => Promise<string>;\n  afterModelResponse?: (session: ChatSession, responseText: string, usage?: UsageMetadata) => Promise<void>;\n  onError?: (session: ChatSession, error: Error) => Promise<void>;\n  /** The supported seam for an EU AI Act Art. 14 human-oversight control: fires before each known tool's executor, and a returned ToolResponse replaces the call (see docs/adding-a-tool.md); it receives the model's raw tool input and, through session, the caller's authHeaders, so a policy that logs either logs personal data and credential material; the engine asserts nothing about any policy installed here. */\n  beforeToolCall?: (session: ChatSession, call: ToolCall) => Promise<ToolResponse | undefined>;\n  /** Fires when the model asks for a tool round the budget refuses, after the last provider call and before afterModelResponse; a returned string reaches the user as the turn's closing text, and the engine writes none of its own (see specs/hal-engine-tool-budget/spec.md). */\n  onToolBudgetExhausted?: (session: ChatSession, budget: ToolBudgetInfo) => Promise<string | undefined>;\n}\n\nexport interface ToolBudgetInfo {\n  maxToolRounds: number;\n  requestedTools: string[];\n}\n\ntype BeforeToolCall = NonNullable<OrchestratorHooks['beforeToolCall']>;\ntype OnToolBudgetExhausted = NonNullable<OrchestratorHooks['onToolBudgetExhausted']>;\n\nexport const TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED = 'tool_budget_exhausted';\n\nexport interface ChatOrchestrator {\n  processMessage(session: ChatSession): Promise<string>;\n  processMessageStream(session: ChatSession): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk>;\n}\n\nexport interface ChatOrchestratorOptions {\n  maxToolRounds?: number;\n  contextConfig?: Partial<ContextConfig>;\n  promptBuilderOptions?: PromptBuilderOptions;\n  hooks?: OrchestratorHooks;\n  sessionStore?: SessionStore;\n}\n\ninterface ToolExecutionResult {\n  clientMessages: OutgoingMessage[];\n  suppressOutput: boolean;\n}\nexport function createChatOrchestrator(\n  provider: AIProvider,\n  promptBuilder: PromptBuilder,\n  toolRegistry?: ToolRegistry,\n  options?: ChatOrchestratorOptions\n): ChatOrchestrator {\n  const maxToolRounds = normalizeToolRounds(options?.maxToolRounds);\n  const contextConfig = createContextConfig(options?.contextConfig);\n  const toolInstructions = toolRegistry?.getPromptInstructions();\n  const baseSystemPrompt = promptBuilder.build({...options?.promptBuilderOptions, toolInstructions});\n  const tools = toolRegistry?.getDefinitions();\n  const hooks = options?.hooks;\n  const sessionStore = options?.sessionStore;\n\n  return {\n    async processMessage(session: ChatSession): Promise<string> {\n      return collectText(this.processMessageStream(session));\n    },\n\n    async *processMessageStream(session: ChatSession): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk> {\n      try {\n        if (hooks?.beforeSession) await hooks.beforeSession(session);\n\n        let userMessage = lastUserContent(session);\n\n        if (hooks?.beforeUserInput) {\n          userMessage = await rewriteUserMessage(session, userMessage, hooks.beforeUserInput);\n        }\n\n        if (hooks?.afterUserInput) await hooks.afterUserInput(session, userMessage);\n\n        const systemPrompt = hooks?.beforeModelResponse\n          ? await hooks.beforeModelResponse(session, baseSystemPrompt)\n          : baseSystemPrompt;\n\n        const messages: Message[] = toMessages(session.entries, contextConfig);\n        let lastUsage: UsageMetadata | undefined;\n        let responseText = '';\n\n        // Bounded by the budget gate below, not the header: maxToolRounds executed rounds, maxToolRounds + 1 provider calls.\n        for (let round = 0; ; round++) {\n          log.info('orchestrator', 'starting round', {round});\n          const pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[] = [];\n\n          const outcome = yield* streamRound(provider, {messages, systemPrompt, tools}, pendingToolCalls);\n          responseText += outcome.text;\n          lastUsage = outcome.usage ?? lastUsage;\n\n          if (!shouldContinueToolLoop(outcome.stopReason, pendingToolCalls, toolRegistry)) break;\n          if (budgetSpent(round, maxToolRounds, pendingToolCalls)) {\n            const budget = {maxToolRounds, requestedTools: pendingToolCalls.map(tc => tc.name)};\n            responseText += yield* closeExhaustedTurn(session, budget, hooks?.onToolBudgetExhausted);\n            break;\n          }\n\n          log.info('orchestrator', 'executing tools', {tools: pendingToolCalls.map(tc => tc.name)});\n          const {clientMessages, suppressOutput} = await executeToolCalls(\n            pendingToolCalls,\n            messages,\n            session,\n            toolCallRunner(toolRegistry!, session, hooks?.beforeToolCall)\n          );\n          log.info('orchestrator', 'tool execution complete', {\n            clientMessageCount: clientMessages.length,\n            clientMessageTypes: clientMessages.map(m => m.type),\n            suppressOutput,\n          });\n\n          if (clientMessages.length > 0) {\n            yield {type: 'tool_result' as const, clientMessages};\n          }\n\n          if (suppressOutput) {\n            yield {type: 'suppress_output' as const};\n          }\n        }\n\n        if (hooks?.afterModelResponse) await hooks.afterModelResponse(session, responseText, lastUsage);\n      } catch (error) {\n        if (hooks?.onError && error instanceof Error) {\n          await hooks.onError(session, error);\n        }\n        throw error;\n      } finally {\n        // Before both: a provider that threw mid-stream leaves an entry open, and nothing should persist it that way.\n        commitStreamingEntries(session);\n        try {\n          if (hooks?.afterSession) await hooks.afterSession(session);\n        } finally {\n          await saveSession(sessionStore, session);\n        }\n      }\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n// A negative budget means no tool rounds; a non-finite one, NaN included, falls back to the default rather than never ending.\nfunction normalizeToolRounds(value: number | undefined): number {\n  if (value === undefined || !Number.isFinite(value)) return DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS;\n  return Math.max(0, Math.floor(value));\n}\n\n// Gates execution, not the provider call: a round nobody can read is never run, and the call that read the last one already has.\nfunction budgetSpent(round: number, maxToolRounds: number, pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[]): boolean {\n  if (round < maxToolRounds) return false;\n  log.warn('orchestrator', 'tool budget exhausted', {\n    maxToolRounds,\n    requestedTools: pendingToolCalls.map(tc => tc.name),\n  });\n  return true;\n}\n\n// The stop always follows the text: the round's own stop already committed its entry, and a bare text chunk would open one never committed.\nasync function* closeExhaustedTurn(\n  session: ChatSession,\n  budget: ToolBudgetInfo,\n  hook?: OnToolBudgetExhausted\n): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk, string> {\n  const sentence = (hook ? await hook(session, budget) : undefined) ?? '';\n  if (sentence !== '') yield {type: 'text', text: sentence};\n  yield {type: 'stop', stopReason: TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED};\n  return sentence;\n}\n\ninterface RoundOutcome {\n  stopReason: string;\n  text: string;\n  usage?: UsageMetadata;\n}\n\n// `yield*` in the caller evaluates to this return, so the round's totals come back as data.\nasync function* streamRound(\n  provider: AIProvider,\n  request: SendMessageParams,\n  pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[]\n): AsyncGenerator<MessageChunk, RoundOutcome> {\n  let stopReason = '';\n  let text = '';\n  let usage: UsageMetadata | undefined;\n\n  for await (const chunk of provider.sendMessage(request)) {\n    yield chunk;\n    collectToolCalls(chunk, pendingToolCalls);\n    stopReason = extractStopReason(chunk) ?? stopReason;\n    usage = extractUsage(chunk) ?? usage;\n    if (chunk.type === 'text') text += chunk.text;\n  }\n\n  return {stopReason, text, usage};\n}\n\n// A changed message is also written back to the session's last user entry.\nasync function rewriteUserMessage(\n  session: ChatSession,\n  userMessage: string,\n  hook: (session: ChatSession, userMessage: string) => Promise<string>\n): Promise<string> {\n  const modified = await hook(session, userMessage);\n  if (modified === userMessage) return userMessage;\n\n  updateLastUserContent(session, modified);\n  return modified;\n}\n\nfunction lastUserContent(session: ChatSession): string {\n  for (let i = session.entries.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\n    const entry = session.entries[i];\n    if (entry.role === 'user') return entry.content;\n  }\n  return '';\n}\n\nfunction updateLastUserContent(session: ChatSession, content: string): void {\n  for (let i = session.entries.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\n    const entry = session.entries[i];\n    if (entry.role === 'user') {\n      entry.content = content;\n      return;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// One runner per round: the policy sees each known call before its executor, and an unknown name keeps the registry's own path.\nfunction toolCallRunner(toolRegistry: ToolRegistry, session: ChatSession, beforeToolCall?: BeforeToolCall) {\n  const context: ToolContext = {\n    userId: session.userId,\n    sessionId: session.sessionId,\n    workspaceId: session.workspaceId,\n    authHeaders: session.authHeaders,\n  };\n\n  return async (tc: ToolCall): Promise<ToolResponse> => {\n    const declined =\n      beforeToolCall && toolRegistry.has(tc.name) ? await consultPolicy(beforeToolCall, session, tc) : undefined;\n    return declined ?? toolRegistry.execute(tc.name, tc.input, context);\n  };\n}\n\n// A throwing policy declines its call rather than failing the turn: every tool_use must still be answered.\nasync function consultPolicy(\n  policy: BeforeToolCall,\n  session: ChatSession,\n  tc: ToolCall\n): Promise<ToolResponse | undefined> {\n  try {\n    return await policy(session, tc);\n  } catch (error) {\n    log.warn('orchestrator', 'beforeToolCall threw, so the call is declined', {\n      tool: tc.name,\n      errorType: error instanceof Error ? error.name : typeof error,\n    });\n    return {result: `The ${tc.name} call was declined and not performed.`};\n  }\n}\n\nasync function executeToolCalls(\n  pendingToolCalls: ToolCall[],\n  messages: Message[],\n  session: ChatSession,\n  runToolCall: (tc: ToolCall) => Promise<ToolResponse>\n): Promise<ToolExecutionResult> {\n  messages.push(buildToolCallMessages(pendingToolCalls));\n\n  const responses = await Promise.all(\n    pendingToolCalls.map(async tc => {\n      const response = await runToolCall(tc);\n      return {tc, response};\n    })\n  );\n\n  const toolResults: ToolResultContent[] = responses.map(({tc, response}) => ({\n    type: 'tool_result' as const,\n    toolUseId: tc.id,\n    content: response.result,\n  }));\n  messages.push({role: 'user', content: toolResults});\n\n  const rawClientMessages = responses.flatMap(({response}) => response.clientMessages ?? []);\n\n  return {\n    clientMessages: assignEntryIndices(rawClientMessages, session),\n    suppressOutput: responses.some(({response}) => response.suppressAssistantResponse === true),\n  };\n}\n\nfunction assignEntryIndices(clientMessages: OutgoingMessage[], session: ChatSession): OutgoingMessage[] {\n  return clientMessages.map(msg => {\n    if (msg.type !== 'entry_upsert') return msg;\n    const index = appendEntry(session, msg.entry);\n    return {...msg, index};\n  });\n}\n\n// A store outage is not a reason to fail the turn the customer already received.\nasync function saveSession(store: SessionStore | undefined, session: ChatSession): Promise<void> {\n  if (!store?.save) return;\n\n  try {\n    await store.save(session);\n  } catch (error) {\n    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    log.error('orchestrator', 'session save failed', {sessionId: session.sessionId, error: message});\n  }\n}\n","numLines":335,"startLine":1,"totalLines":335}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_38470b62c3244504b3ba95c6","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_229fb827d5c44da09471bc2a","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n2\t\n3\t| Field  | Value                |\n4\t| ------ | -------------------- |\n5\t| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n6\t| Status | Shipped              |\n7\t\n8\t`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n9\t\n10\t## Installing hooks through config\n11\t\n12\t- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n13\t- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n14\t- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n15\t- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n16\t- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n17\t- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n18\t\n19\t## Engine startup\n20\t\n21\t- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n22\t\n23\t## Measured semantics — out of scope\n24\t\n25\tThese properties of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` record the measured semantics that hook features specify against. The `onError` boundary remains out of scope; issue #93 defines the aggregate usage delivered after tool rounds.\n26\t\n27\t- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n28\t- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the summed provider-call usage for the entire turn, so tool rounds are included in a deployer's billing or audit total ([validated by: reports the summed usage from every provider call in a turn with two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestratorUsage.acceptance.test.ts#L44)).\n29\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"04691e8d-bf30-4680-addf-c60a90bc5e36","uuid":"d3df4a1c-8f8e-425b-9d4f-13de8ee48c5a","timestamp":"2026-11-02T18:58:49.910Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/d7d43383fd97/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-config-hooks/spec.md","content":"# Config-Installed Orchestrator Hooks\n\n| Field  | Value                |\n| ------ | -------------------- |\n| Issue  | re-cinq/HALEngine#36 |\n| Status | Shipped              |\n\n`OrchestratorHooks` is the engine's only lifecycle seam, and `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` is the supported way to install it: `createHalEngine` forwards the set to `createChatOrchestrator`, so a hook a deployer passes through the factory reaches the running orchestrator rather than sitting unread on the config object. This spec pins that forwarding and the measured semantics the seam already carries, so the hook-based features that follow in this epic — transparency disclosure, audit and usage recording, human oversight, provider failover — specify against real behaviour rather than against what the hook names suggest.\n\n## Installing hooks through config\n\n- A hook passed through `orchestrator.hooks` is forwarded to the orchestrator and fires ([validated by: forwards an orchestrator hook, so one passed through the config actually fires](../../src/config.test.ts#L19)).\n- An engine built with no `orchestrator` key at all still processes a message, so the field is optional in fact and not only in the type ([validated by: processes a message when the config declares no orchestrator key at all](../../src/config.test.ts#L95)).\n- A `beforeModelResponse` hook installed through config is handed the base system prompt built from `prompt`, proving the hook reaches the orchestrator and is wired into the prompt pipeline rather than only being stored on the config ([validated by: hands a config-installed beforeModelResponse hook the built base system prompt, not merely storing it](../../src/config.test.ts#L108)).\n- The value the hook returns then replaces the system prompt the provider receives; this is exercised at the orchestrator layer because `HalEngineConfig.provider` takes a `ProviderConfig` and offers no seam to inject a recording provider through config ([validated by: replaces system prompt with returned value](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L221)).\n- For a session that completes without error the hooks fire in the order `beforeSession` → `beforeUserInput` → `afterUserInput` → `beforeModelResponse` → `afterModelResponse` → `afterSession` ([validated by: fires the lifecycle hooks in documented order for a session that completes without error](../../src/config.test.ts#L132)).\n- A `beforeSession` that throws reaches the caller as a rejection, and also runs `onError` with the thrown error and then `afterSession`, because the call sits inside the same `try`/`finally` as the rest of the turn. An `afterSession` hook called under this condition can infer the session never advanced past `beforeSession`: no user-input hooks ran, no model response was generated, and no tool rounds were started — a cleanup hook that allocates in `beforeSession` and frees in `afterSession` will therefore always see the allocation and the release on the same error path ([validated by: runs onError then afterSession when it throws, and still rejects to the caller](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.test.ts#L116)).\n\n## Engine startup\n\n- The engine's `start()` method rejects the returned promise when it cannot bind the configured port, rather than emitting an unhandled error event that would take the process down ([validated by: rejects instead of taking the process down with an unhandled error event](../../src/config.test.ts#L174)).\n\n## Measured semantics — out of scope\n\nThese properties of `src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts` record the measured semantics that hook features specify against. The `onError` boundary remains out of scope; issue #93 defines the aggregate usage delivered after tool rounds.\n\n- The `catch` gates `onError` on `error instanceof Error` ([chatOrchestrator.ts#L150](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestrator.ts#L150)), so a rejection that is not an `Error` — a thrown string or object — bypasses `onError` entirely while still propagating to the caller.\n- `afterModelResponse` is called once per user message after the round loop exits and receives the summed provider-call usage for the entire turn, so tool rounds are included in a deployer's billing or audit total ([validated by: reports the summed usage from every provider call in a turn with two tool rounds](../../src/orchestration/chatOrchestratorUsage.acceptance.test.ts#L44)).\n","numLines":29,"startLine":1,"totalLines":29}},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"call_229fb827d5c44da09471bc2a","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_905ebc012be847e099d1aa8b","type":"tool_result","content":"dod.md\ntest-commands.yml\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). Entries are written for somebody installing the\npackage, not for somebody reading this repository's commit log.\n\n## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- `SessionStore` gains an optional `save(session)` member: the write signal a durable store needs. The engine calls it once per processed user message, from the same `finally` path that runs `afterSession`, so it fires for a turn that ended in a provider error as well as one that succeeded — a store that only heard about successes would lose exactly the conversations a customer complains about. By the time it runs, the turn's entries are committed, so the assistant entry's `isStreaming` is already `false` — including when a provider threw mid-stream, where the engine now closes the open entry before saving, recording `truncated` on it, rather than persisting a half-streamed entry that looks finished. An `afterSession` hook that throws no longer costs the turn its write signal. What `save` throws or rejects with is caught and logged as one line carrying the session id, and the turn continues: the client still receives its full stream and `stream_end`, because a database outage is not a reason for the agent to stop answering. The member is typed `void | Promise<void>`, so a synchronous implementation and an `async` one both satisfy it. Additive: `InMemorySessionStore` does not implement `save` and is unchanged, and a store that omits it behaves exactly as before.\n- `SessionStore` gains an optional `evict(sessionId)` member, and `InMemorySessionStore` implements it. The engine calls it only for a session whose socket closed while `create` was still pending — one that no client ever received and that has no entries to keep. `InMemorySessionStore` now also takes `{maxAgeMs}` (default `28_800_000`, eight hours), exported as `InMemorySessionStoreOptions`. Expired sessions are evicted when read and swept at the head of `create`, which stops at the first live session, so the bound costs nothing on a busy store. There is no timer: a library-owned interval would keep your process alive.\n- **`MongoSessionStore`**, a durable session store, exported as `MongoSessionStore` and `createMongoSessionStore`. It takes a collection, a client with a database name, or a connection URL — nothing in the package reads the environment or assumes a host. The collection is the truth and the in-process cache is an optimisation: a session written by `save` is readable by a second store on the same collection. `delete` removes the document — it is the consumer's erasure primitive and the engine never calls it — as do `eraseConversation`, `eraseOlderThan(cutoff)` and `eraseAll`, while `clear` alone touches only the cache; `docs/session-stores.md` opens with the table that says which is which, because the previous documentation would have led you to believe `delete` erased. Documents carry `createdAt` and `updatedAt`, so a retention period is expressible without per-entry timestamps. **No credential is ever written**: `authHeaders`, `authorization`, `cookie` and `host` are stripped at any depth, not just the top level, and the same `stripCredentialKeys` is exported for stores you write yourself. `mongodb` is an optional peer dependency loaded only when the store actually connects, so a consumer who installs no optional peer can still import the package root and use the in-memory store. **Migration note**: `delete` and `evict` both erase on this store, so the one-line `onDisconnect: sessionId => store.delete(sessionId)` that restores the pre-0.4 close behaviour destroys the conversation on every socket close — point `onDisconnect` at neither. Nothing needs reclaiming by hand: the in-process cache is bounded by `maxAgeMs` (eight hours by default) and drops stale entries without touching a document. This does **not** deliver conversation resume, and it does not make the engine safe to run as more than one instance against one conversation.\n\n### Changed\n\n- Every `SessionStore` member is now `Awaitable<T>` (`T | Promise<T>`) rather than a bare `T`, so a store backed by a database is expressible for the first time. `Awaitable` is exported from the package root. **A synchronous implementation needs no change** — `T` is assignable to `T | Promise<T>` in a return position — and `InMemorySessionStore` is unedited. The WebSocket connection handler now awaits `create` and the release that follows a close: it pauses the socket across the await so a frame sent before `connected` arrives is buffered rather than dropped, attaches its `close` listener before the await so a socket that closes mid-create leaves no session behind, and catches a rejection from either call so one store failure closes one socket instead of ending the process with an unhandled rejection. **Migration note**: the `connected` frame now arrives one tick later, after the awaited `create`, so a client that waits for it (as the protocol already requires) sees no difference; only code that assumed the frame was emitted synchronously with the upgrade does.\n- **The engine no longer erases a conversation when its socket closes.** The WebSocket close handler called `sessionStore.delete(sessionId)` unconditionally, which meant that behind a durable store every conversation was written and then destroyed the moment the customer closed their tab — the opposite of what this engine's own documentation promised. `SessionStore.delete` now has no engine-internal caller at all and is purely your erasure primitive. The credentials the socket carried are cleared from the session on close, since they were issued for a request that is over; the entries are untouched. **Migration note**: to restore the old behaviour exactly, pass `onDisconnect: sessionId => store.delete(sessionId)` — that one line is the whole migration. If you rely on the default store not growing, note that it is now bounded by `maxAgeMs` (eight hours) rather than by the close handler; that is a memory bound, not a retention policy, and the retention decision remains yours.\n\n### Fixed\n\n- `onError` and `afterSession` hooks now fire when `beforeSession` throws, matching the guarantee the hook names imply. Previously, a throw from `beforeSession` escaped the `try`/`finally` block entirely, so neither hook ran. **Migration note**: if your `afterSession` hook assumed it would only be called after a full session turn, it will now also be called when `beforeSession` itself fails — the hook can inspect the session to detect this case (no entries beyond the initial user message will have been processed).\n\n## [0.3.0] - 2026-09-29\n\n**Upgrading from 0.2.x.** Nothing is removed and no existing signature changes, but check these before you bump:\n\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains two optional members, `beforeToolCall` and `onToolBudgetExhausted`. Code that checks the hooks exhaustively, for example `satisfies Record<keyof OrchestratorHooks, …>`, fails to type-check until it names both.\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` now validates tool input against each tool's `inputSchema`, so a tool that produced its own message for malformed input receives only valid input; the model reads the registry's validation message instead.\n- `maxToolRounds` now counts executed rounds: the default of 5 runs 5 tool rounds, not 6. Raise it by one to keep the old count.\n- Offering a bare access token as the only WebSocket subprotocol still works but is deprecated: offer `hal.v1` beside it now, because the next minor release stops accepting a bare token.\n- New dependencies: `ajv`, and `cookie` moves from `^0.7` to `^2`.\n\n### Added\n\n- `VertexConfig` gains an optional `apiEndpoint` field, forwarded verbatim to the `@google-cloud/vertexai` SDK constructor. Deployers who must keep inference in the EU multi-region can set `apiEndpoint: 'aiplatform.eu.rep.googleapis.com'`; the change is additive and the default behaviour (endpoint derived from `location`) is unchanged.\n- `HalEngineConfig.transport` now accepts three optional extension points: `additionalRoutes` (mounts a router under `basePath`, unauthenticated), `rootRoutes` (mounts a router at `/`, after the `basePath` router and before the 404 catch-all), and `errorHandler` (replaces Express's default HTML error page). Pass none of them and the app is identical to before. See `docs/getting-started.md` for usage and the GDPR / NIS-2 notes that apply to unauthenticated routes.\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains an optional `beforeToolCall(session, call)` hook that fires before each known tool's executor, once per call per round. Return `undefined` to let the call run, or a `ToolResponse` to decline it: the executor is skipped and the model reads your `result` as that call's `tool_result`, with `clientMessages` and `suppressAssistantResponse` behaving as a tool's would. A hook that throws declines the call instead of failing the turn. Use it for an audit of what the model attempted or an EU AI Act Article 14 human-oversight control. The hook receives the model's raw tool input and, through `session`, the caller's `authHeaders`, so don't log either. Additive: with no hook installed, nothing changes.\n- `OrchestratorHooks` gains an optional `onToolBudgetExhausted(session, budget)` hook, and `ToolBudgetInfo` (`{maxToolRounds, requestedTools}`) and `TOOL_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` are exported. It fires when the model asks for a tool round `maxToolRounds` refuses, after the last provider call and before `afterModelResponse`. Return a sentence and the turn ends on it as an ordinary assistant entry, so the user sees a degraded answer instead of silence; the engine writes no prose of its own. Every exhausted run now also ends with a `{type: 'stop', stopReason: 'tool_budget_exhausted'}` chunk, a new value for the existing field rather than a new type, which the WebSocket transport sends nothing for. The turn still ends with `stream_end`, never `error`. Additive: with no hook installed, the wire is unchanged.\n- `AssistantEntry` and `ThinkingEntry` gain an optional `truncated: true`, set on an entry the run could not finish: a provider failure mid-stream, or a round that ends on a tool call with no `stop`. The server re-sends such an entry as an `entry_upsert` carrying the flag just before its `entry_commit`, and the flag stays on the stored entry, so a client can render a cut-off answer as cut off live or on a replay, without relying on the transient `error` frame. An entry committed normally never carries it; a suppressed entry is flagged in the session but never re-sent. Text the thinking-tag parser was still holding back is now kept in a cut-off entry, as it already was on a normal stop. Additive: a client that ignores the field renders what it did before, and the extra `entry_upsert` replaces the entry with the content it already has.\n\n### Changed\n\n- Every `user_message` now ends in exactly one `stream_end`, including a run that fails and a message that fails validation; the `error` frame is advisory rather than terminal. Unparseable JSON remains the one frame answered with `INVALID_FORMAT` and no `stream_end`. **Migration note**: `stream_end` may now follow an `error` frame, so treat `stream_end` (or the socket closing) as the only end of a run. The one live consumer handles the two frame types independently, and both clear its processing state, so it keeps working unchanged and no deprecation period is served by delaying.\n- Every assistant and thinking entry a run opens is now committed when the run ends, however it ends: a provider that throws mid-stream, or a round that ends on a tool call with no `stop`, no longer leaves an entry with `isStreaming: true` in the session. The partial content is kept exactly as streamed, and on a throw its `entry_commit` arrives before the `error` frame. **Migration note**: `entry_commit` now arrives on paths that previously sent none, so a client counting commit frames sees more of them. No wire type changes, and a client that already stops streaming on `stream_end` renders the same result.\n- `maxToolRounds` now bounds the tool rounds that are actually executed: at most `maxToolRounds` rounds run, and the provider is called at most `maxToolRounds + 1` times. Previously the loop ran one extra round whose tool results no provider call ever read, so the default of 5 executed 6 rounds; it now executes 5, and the sixth provider call still reads the fifth round's results. `maxToolRounds: 0` now means one provider call and no tool execution, a negative value behaves as 0, and a non-finite value falls back to the default. When the budget runs out, the engine logs one `warn` (`tool budget exhausted`) naming the tools it did not run. **Migration note**: no exported type or signature changes, but a tool with a side effect in that final, unread round no longer runs there; to restore the old count, raise `maxToolRounds` by one.\n- `HalEngineConfig.orchestrator.hooks` now carries a JSDoc comment. The field already shipped\n  in `0.2.x`; the comment makes the lifecycle-hook seam discoverable from the published type","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"04691e8d-bf30-4680-addf-c60a90bc5e36","uuid":"32a77300-aaf2-4ecc-81eb-dddfb8ec9e89","timestamp":"2026-11-02T18:58:57.342Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"dod.md\ntest-commands.yml\n# Changelog\n\nAll notable changes to `@re-cinq/hal-engine` are documented here.\n\nThe format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to\n[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/d7d43383fd97/home-1/.claude/skills/tdd-loop\n\nThis is the authoritative copy of Lore's TDD contract for agent runs. It is checked\ninto the Lore repo at `apps/mcp-server/agent-skills/skills/tdd-loop/` and served to\npods by the gateway's `/skills` registry.\n\nYou run the three phases yourself, in order, in one pass. Do not skip a phase and do\nnot run two facets in one round.\n\n## The three laws\n\n1. Do not write production code unless it is to make a failing test pass.\n2. Do not write more of a unit test than is sufficient to fail — a compile or import\n   failure counts.\n3. Do not write more production code than is sufficient to make the currently failing\n   test pass.\n\n## Phase 1 — RED\n\nDetect the toolchain from the project (`package.json`, `go.mod`, `pyproject.toml`,\n`Cargo.toml`, `pom.xml`, `Gemfile`, `mix.exs`, `Makefile`, CI config) and mirror the\nconventions already in the repo's tests — framework, directory, file suffix, assertion\nstyle. Assume nothing about the language.\n\n**The one-facet rule.** Name the single smallest behaviour that moves you forward. If\nthe behaviour you were handed would force more than one new facet, write the test for\nthe smallest facet only and report the leftovers rather than growing the round.\n\nWrite **exactly one** test. Run it. Confirm it fails **for the reason you expect** —\nthe behaviour is absent, not the import path typo'd or a fixture missing. A test you\ndid not run is not a red bar, it is a guess.\n\nIf a behaviour would not change the code under test at all, say so and write nothing.\nA test that forces no change is not worth writing.\n\n### No mocks, no stubs\n\nDesign every test to exercise pure logic with real values — value in, value out, not\ninteractions. A facet that seems to *require* a double is a design signal: the real\nproblem is an unmanaged side effect, not the absence of a mock. Pick a facet that is\ntestable without doubles, and note that the production code should push the I/O to the\nedges so the pure core is directly testable.\n\n- Stubbed lookup → pure function on a value. `discountFor(user)` taking a real `User`,\n  not `discountFor(userId)` fetching one behind a `UserRepository` stub.\n- Interaction mock → assert on a returned value. Have the core return the intent and\n  assert `notificationsFor(order)`; sending happens at the edge.\n- Clock/RNG stub → inject the value. `isExpired(deadline, now)`, both plain values.\n\nA true collaborator boundary that must eventually be pinned down is a separate\n**contract test**, never an ad-hoc mock sprinkled into a unit test.\n\n### Test naming\n\nThe principle is language-agnostic; the surface form follows the language.\n\n- Short, clear, scannable. The name carries the expected behaviour **and** the tested\n  data — never the implementation.\n- No \"should\".\n- Apply the *ask-why* check: a name that does not answer why the test exists is a\n  structural name, not an intent-revealing one.\n- Include the context only when the enclosing suite does not already establish it.\n\nGood: `returns 0 for an empty list`, `returns I for 1`, `throws RangeError for 0`.\nBad: `should return zero`, `test sum`, `works correctly`.\n\n## Phase 2 — GREEN\n\nWrite the fewest lines that turn the bar green. No extra features, no speculative\ngenerality, no refactoring yet.\n\n**Kent Beck's green-bar strategies:**\n\n- *Obvious implementation* — when the real code is trivial and unmistakable, write it.\n- *Fake it* — when it is not, return a constant and let a later test replace constants\n  with variables.\n- *Triangulate* — leave generalization to the NEXT test. Do not guess the general\n  solution now.\n\nRun the tests you are working against — the one you just wrote plus the acceptance\ntests that define done — by naming their files. Never the repository's whole suite:\nCI runs that on every push, and the pod's Bash hook refuses a bare `npm test` or\n`vitest run` with no path. All of what you run must pass.\n\n**Never edit the test to make it pass.** If the test is wrong, say so and stop; that is\na finding, not a green bar.\n\n### Where code goes\n\nNever create code in catch-all `utils` / `misc` / `helpers` / `common` / `shared` /\n`lib` modules. Place and name code by the domain concept it serves — discount logic in\n`pricing/`, not `utils/calc.ts`. When no home exists, create a domain-named module,\nnever a generic bucket.\n\n### Naming\n\nMeaningful but concise — one to three words (`pendingOrders`, `isContainer`,\n`retryCount`). No single letters except trivial loop indices; no `temp`/`data`/`x`; no\nsprawling four-plus-word names. Name by role: `rawInput`, `normalized`.\n\n## Phase 3 — REFACTOR\n\nThe bar is green. Improve the design without changing behaviour, and fit the new code\ninto the surrounding architecture. The suite stays green throughout.\n\n**Duplication is duplicated knowledge, not just duplicated text.** Hunt for and\ncollapse: the same business rule expressed two ways, a constant or validation\nre-derived in two places, parallel structures encoding one concept, a domain concept\nnamed differently across modules, the same idea living in both code and a test.\n\nRefactor **both** production and test code. Test refactoring counts: collapsing\nduplicated tests into one parameterized test, removing now-subsumed tests, extracting\nshared setup, clarifying names. Behaviour coverage must not shrink.\n\n### The Simple Design Dynamo\n\nReuse is a **result** of removing duplication, never a prediction. Never extract because\nsomething \"might\" be reused. For each candidate, in order:\n\n1. Remove the duplication — collapse the copies into one. The reusable shape only\n   becomes visible after the duplication is gone.\n2. Separate what varies from what stays the same.\n3. Extract a template — the algorithm steps with explicit variation points.\n4. Name each operation concretely. **If a step cannot be named, the variation is not\n   understood yet — stop and look.**\n5. Parameterize the details — lift literals into arguments. Generic logic in code,\n   specific values in data passed in.\n\nThis loops: removing duplication creates structures → you name them → better names\nraise cohesion → higher cohesion makes the next, bigger duplication visible.\n\n**Rule of three.** Two copies = notice, and remove the duplication *in place*. Promote\nto a shared module only on the third genuinely similar case. Lifting on the second copy\ncouples unrelated callers and is harder to undo than the duplication was.\n\n### Reveal intent\n\nFor any name you touch, ask \"why does this exist / what is it really?\" — the name must\nanswer that, not describe the mechanism. A function name containing \"and\"\n(`parseAndStore`) is two responsibilities; split it. Improve names iteratively:\nnonsense → structurally accurate → intent-revealing.\n\n### Fewest elements\n\nInline indirection that does not pull its weight — a wrapper that only forwards. Apply\n\"too simple to break\": never add a test for trivial composition of already-trusted\nparts. Reject premature abstraction.\n\n### Refactor discipline — this is where rounds go wrong\n\n- **Never change production code and test code in the same step.** Each step touches\n  only tests or only production code, then run the suite to green before the next.\n  Changing both at once means a green bar no longer proves the refactor was safe.\n- One target per step, always.\n- **If a step turns the suite red, revert that step** rather than chasing the failure.\n- Grep for existing abstractions to reuse before inventing new ones.\n- May move or extract code and create files, but never add behaviour or expand scope\n  beyond the current test.\n\n### Out-of-scope smells\n\nWhile you have the context loaded, note architecture and code smells you spot that are\n**unrelated** to the current round — god objects, leaky abstractions, missing seams,\ndead code. **Do not fix them**; that blows the round's scope. Collect them as\n`future_improvements`: location + smell + one-line suggested fix. Report them; act on\nnone.\n\n## Ending the round\n\nCommit and push per the delivery contract in your task instructions — in a pod, the\nwork dies with the container otherwise. Commit only on green; a red suite is never\npushed.\n\nReport: the facet you closed, the facet you would take next, the post-refactor test\nresult, and the `future_improvements` list.\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"04691e8d-bf30-4680-addf-c60a90bc5e36","uuid":"73eb12be-5e7b-4f7e-9334-8a6a31c3a70d","timestamp":"2026-11-02T18:59:13.629Z","isSynthetic":true}
